### U-011-183
- **tipo:** historia
- **titulo:** A comment calling entrepreneurship overrated triggered his defenses — he turned it into a video
- **desarrollo:** On a YouTube community post asking "what is one thing that you find overrated but most people love," the top comment said entrepreneurship: great if you have the skills, but "work is work"; "I'm sick of being told I'm a wage slave for choosing to work a nine to five; most of these entrepreneurs are really just unemployed." He calls it "very loaded, very assumptive," but understands the assumption from seeing people flex big numbers online. "This irked me at first... my defense systems immediately started going off," creating objections in his mind. He hates getting trapped in social comments "but our brain loves it." So he turned it into a video, questioning how people can't realize entrepreneurship is the only path to full control over lifestyle, which is "the only way to have full control over your enjoyment."
- **ejemplos:** The YouTube comment.
- **cita:** "my defense systems immediately started going off"
- **terminos:** defense systems; full control over your lifestyle
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** complementario
- **prerrequisitos:** ninguno
- **fuente:** Self Improvers Are Creating Their Own Careers (The New Economy).md, 2:29–3:53, 2023-04-16
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-011-185
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Objection 1: "People hate what they don't understand" — you see <1% of a creator's life
- **desarrollo:** Commenters haven't been entrepreneurs; they don't understand "the nuances of the brain that you pick up through daily experience." Online you see maybe 1% or less of someone's life ("you don't know me at all" despite the authenticity trend), or 5% of their business, and think it's enough to draw conclusions without experience. Observing society should correct your own actions, not reinforce what you've been told and justify it as "I chose to do this" when tied to money. "They think entrepreneurship is overrated because they aren't entrepreneurs."
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "people hate what they don't understand"
- **terminos:** ninguno
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-011-183
- **fuente:** Self Improvers Are Creating Their Own Careers (The New Economy).md, 4:41–5:26, 2023-04-16
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-011-186
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Objection 2: "wage slave" — if losing the job means you can't pay bills, you're by definition a modern slave
- **desarrollo:** "Your feelings aren't reality: if you got let go and you could not pay the bills then you are by definition a modern slave." "Slave doesn't have to be good or bad": we agree past slavery was bad, but he argues "we will look back on the Industrial Age and realize that slavery was the norm and that is what AI is coming in to fix." Forced to work for survival, you lack choice; your choices align with unconscious and subconscious survival goals. Most are unconscious of what happens behind the scenes at their company (exploitation, paying pennies to third-world sweatshops — "a much worse form of slavery"); becoming conscious of this and continuing is "a terrible thing." Caveat: not all companies; but mega-corporations or chains like McDonald's serve "the worst food for health." You can't control this at the start — that's the self-actualization journey; helping create a better world makes life meaningful.
- **ejemplos:** Sweatshops; McDonald's.
- **cita:** "we will look back on the Industrial Age and realize that it slavery was the norm and that is what AI is coming in to fix"
- **terminos:** modern slave; wage slave; survival goals
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-011-183
- **fuente:** Self Improvers Are Creating Their Own Careers (The New Economy).md, 5:26–6:58, 2023-04-16
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-012-203
- **tipo:** definición
- **titulo:** Wage slavery: meaningless grind work you didn't choose, for someone else, just to survive
- **desarrollo:** "Wage slavery is doing meaningless grind work you didn't choose for someone else just to survive." He is not anti-job: jobs are "very worthy stepping stones for skill acquisition and experience". To the person who says "I actually like my job": he's not speaking to them, and partially thinks they're lying "to avoid facing your potential while simultaneously being unconscious of your potential". He speaks to those who understand the psychology of true enjoyment and can't stand one-third of life doing unchosen work, one-third mentally exhausted, one-third asleep, for 40+ years.
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "Wage slavery is doing meaningless grind work you didn't choose for someone else just to survive."
- **terminos:** wage slavery, stepping stones

- **nivel:** fundamental

- **tension:** ninguna


### U-012-210
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Slaves don't know they're slaves: the financial definition of slavery and why identification with your job triggers a threat response
- **desarrollo:** "The problem is that slaves don't know their slaves." This goes beyond wage slavery: "we are all slaves, usually to ideology and belief systems". Slavery is about force; we think of the physical version, but wage slavery is financial: "if you cannot stop showing up to work without catastrophe and if you have no skills to create alternatives, you meet the definition of a slave, no matter if your feelings tell you otherwise". Worse, if you've identified with your job, you'll take this as an attack: your identity, "your mental body", trying to preserve its survival; you feel a threat response with no physical threat present; wanting to argue "only proves my point further".

- **origen:** propia

## CLÚSTER C-T15-09: The employer controls everything that pays the bills
- idea: Solo obtienes control total sobre tu tiempo e ingreso cuando entiendes que el emprendimiento es el único camino para ello: en un empleo no controlas el producto, la tarea ni la adquisición de clientes; el empleador controla todo lo que paga las cuentas y, por la cadena dinero → supervivencia → vacaciones, energía, nutrición, sueño, controla toda tu vida. Cuánto ganas depende del "vessel" que eliges para tu habilidad, y el empleo pone un techo duro. Un empleo es trabajo desagradable para otro con el único fin de ganar dinero, un mecanismo de supervivencia que, como la escuela, sobrevive gracias al buen marketing. Por eso empezar un negocio "no es opcional" si piensas en lo que quieres: es lo único que da control sobre el tiempo; no es un capricho capitalista sino la forma de resolver problemas, construir algo propio y contribuir mediante intercambio de valor. Dickie Bush lo vivió como la búsqueda de un modelo donde "inputs directly lead to outputs"; el autor, como la observación de que, si otros lo lograron, hay una serie de pasos para controlar el dinero y con él el día, la mente y el tiempo.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: U-016-019 (en C-T15-11) matiza U-016-018 en el mismo video: hay grados de control del empleador. El dato del 25% (U-011-191, 2023) convive con otras proporciones ("un tercio de tu vida es trabajo", U-008-084; "one-third... for 40+ years", U-012-203).
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-016-018
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Step 1: at a job you control neither the product, the task, nor the customer acquisition, so your employer controls everything that pays the bills
- **desarrollo:** "You only gain full control over your time and income when you realize that entrepreneurship is the only path to do so." At a job "you are not in charge of the product, the task you are assigned or the customer acquisition process"; "your employer is in charge of everything that pays the bills, therefore they are in charge of your entire life." The chain: every action you take is tied to money, and money is tied to survival. Your decision to go on vacation depends on your time and money; your energy depends on fitness, rest and nutrition; if you can't afford proper nutrition, don't have time to cook quality meals, or work so much that stress keeps you from sleeping, "it just gradually ruins your life." Hence "your employer controls your entire life."
- **ejemplos:** Vacation dependent on time and money; energy dependent on nutrition and sleep that a stressful job erodes.
- **cita:** "your employer is in charge of everything that pays the bills therefore they are in charge of your entire life"
- **terminos:** ninguno
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-016-017
- **fuente:** Working Hard Won't Make You Rich (Do This Instead).md, 7:38-9:04, 2024-03-31
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-016-048

- **titulo:** How much you earn depends on the vessel you choose for your skill; a job sets a hard limit and is rarely the best long-term vessel
- **desarrollo:** Second realization: "you learn the skills that would make you the most money but realize that you have a hard limit on your earning potential that your employer sets for you." "How much you earn depends on the vessel you choose for that skill, and a job usually isn't the best long-term vessel." The skill is the same; the container (job, freelancing, own business) determines the ceiling.
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "how much you earn depends on the vessel you choose for that skill and a job usually isn't the best long-term vessel"
- **terminos:** vessel; hard limit on your earning potential
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-016-047
- **fuente:** How Smart Creatives Work Less & Earn More LEVERAGE.md, 3:13-3:55, 2024-05-26
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-002-020
- **tipo:** principio
- **titulo:** Seek control over your income: a model where inputs directly lead to outputs
- **desarrollo:** Dickie's motivation for building Ship 30: he worked 14–15 hour days on Wall Street, learning as much as he could, but "there was literally no way I could have done one tenth the effort" and earned differently; people who worked way harder or way less "made the same." That never sat well. He "sought the ability to have control over my income again where inputs directly led to outputs" — getting back to being a tutor, in a sense. Ship 30's original goal was modest: $10,000 in a year (an extra ~$1,000/month, more than he was saving), which he calls "a dream" — contrasting with people assuming multi-seven-figure founders started with big ambition.
- **ejemplos:** Ship 30's $10k-in-a-year goal.
- **cita:** "I sought the ability to have control over my income again where inputs directly led to outputs"
- **terminos:** inputs and outputs; side hustle
- **origen:** de-tercero:Dickie Bush
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-002-019
- **fuente:** Dan Koe & Dickie Bush On One-Person Businesses, Creative Workflows, and Lifestyle Design.md, [41:39]–[42:26], 2023-03-16
- **tension:** ninguna

- **titulo:** If others have done it, there is a series of steps you can take; control over money gives control over your day, routine, mind, emotions and time
- **desarrollo:** "I could just observe. I could just see that other people had a life that I could potentially want. And if other people had done it, then that means there is a series of steps that I can personally take," especially with technology, the internet and social media, "to achieve close to the same thing; not even the full thing, but I could at least have some control over my money. Therefore I could have some control over my day, my routine, my mind, my emotions, and especially my time."
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "if other people had done it then that means there is a series of steps that I can personally take"
- **terminos:** ninguno

- **fuente:** What It Takes To Get Rich In Your 20s.md, 1:24, 2025-08-03
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-018-092
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Self-confidence step 2: start a business; it's the only thing that gives control over your time (a job is a stepping stone)
- **desarrollo:** "If you truly think about what you want in life, starting a business is not optional. A business is the only thing that gives you control over your time." With a job "you're going to be destined to pursuing the goals that are assigned to you for the rest of your life." Dan clarifies: "I'm not saying jobs are bad, I've never said that"; a job is "a stepping stone to learn and acquire skills to eventually be able to go and do your own thing and survive along the way." The problem: becoming trapped because it's comfortable, then locked in by responsibilities (family, bills, mortgage) with no way out, then justifying "that that's what they wanted their entire life." What you really want is what most people want by nature: "autonomy, work you enjoy and to have an impact on the world" to fulfill "your human need to be a valuable contribution to humanity." "A business is not some capitalistic pursuit to own a private jet and mansion; a business is how you solve problems, how you build something of your own, how you contribute to humanity through value exchange, how you take back control of your life, how you develop self-confidence in your interest and expertise."
- **ejemplos:** People locked into a job by family, bills and mortgage.
- **cita:** "a business is how you contribute to humanity through value exchange"
- **terminos:** stepping stone; value exchange; autonomy
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-018-091
- **fuente:** It’s actually pretty easy to be self-disciplined (if you stop getting distracted).md, 16:54-18:19, 2024-05-12
- **tension:** ninguna

- **desarrollo:** "Work consumes about 25 percent of your life if you do the math." Would you rather spend it making discoveries in the unknown with meaning, or predictable sameness — traveling only briefly because you must go back, things out of your control?

### U-016-113

- **titulo:** A job is unpleasant work for someone else for the sole purpose of making money, a survival mechanism; like schools, it survives on good marketing despite a bad product
- **desarrollo:** "A job is some unpleasant work you do for someone else for the sole purpose of making money. A job is a survival mechanism. A job is one milestone on the path to living up to those who shaped your mind." "A job is similar to schools from the perspective that good marketing can make up for a bad product": "after centuries of failing to get results, things like schools are still alive and well for the simple reason that very few people go through the trouble of thinking for themselves." "Most people do what most people do. Most people aren't okay with the results of most people, but by that point it's much harder to escape."
- **ejemplos:** Schools surviving centuries of poor results through marketing.

## CLÚSTER C-T15-14: The path of uncertainty vs the eternal known
- idea: En un 9-to-5, en el freelance, en la agencia o en cualquier "new digital nine to five" vives en el "eternal known": no puedes hacer descubrimientos ni cazar, te aburres, te deprimes y solo obtienes dopamina de fuentes superficiales. El emprendimiento es "the path of uncertainty, like slashing your way through the jungle": exige aprender habilidades que no se enseñan en la escuela, aceptar el fracaso, el rechazo y el progreso lento, aprender de los errores y volver al día siguiente hasta encontrar oro, "required in other words to be human". Paradójicamente es más seguro que el cubículo, que es "the least safe and least secure path... for your psyche". Por eso el emprendimiento es "the only logical option for long-term thinkers": la mente busca desafío, las escaleras, las vacaciones y la jubilación aburren, y la amenaza hoy es psicológica y espiritual, no física.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: el pasaje del "path of uncertainty" se reutiliza casi igual en 2023, 2024 y 2025 con añadidos distintos; la fórmula se invierte de "long-term option for logical thinkers" (2023) a "logical option for long-term thinkers" (2025).
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-008-111
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Entrepreneurship is uncertain but more secure than the cubicle, which is the least safe path for your psyche
- **desarrollo:** Education businesses are the future of schooling and a good opportunity to bank your future on — more secure, though uncertain. Entrepreneurship is uncertain: "that's what meaningful living is, being constantly exposed to novel experiences," and entrepreneurship is the vessel for that. Your ancestors were entrepreneurs serving their community. Robotic living in a cubicle, the same day in and out, is not safe or secure — "the least safe and least secure path you can take especially for your psyche and your mental health."
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "that's what meaningful living is is being constantly exposed to novel experiences entrepreneurship is the vessel to do that"
- **terminos:** meaningful living; vessel
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-008-009
- **fuente:** Zero To $1 Million As A One-Person Business (Working 2-4 Hours A Day).md, 6:40-7:31, 2024-02-25
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-016-208
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** In a 9-to-5 or any "digital 9-to-5" you live in the eternal known, where you can't hunt; entrepreneurship is the path of uncertainty, slashing through the jungle
- **desarrollo:** "When you get stuck in a nine to five or freelance work or agency work or any of the new digital nine to fives, you start to live in the eternal known. You can't make new discoveries in the known; you can't hunt; you get bored, depressed and see life as meaningless, because the only dopamine you get is from superficial sources." "You never take risks, push into the unknown and discover new knowledge, tools and potentials that raise dopamine levels in your brain. You must evolve." "Entrepreneurship is the path of uncertainty, like slashing your way through the jungle": "you are required to learn skills that aren't taught in schools; to be okay with failure, rejection and slow progress; to learn from your mistakes, show up again tomorrow and push until you strike gold."
- **ejemplos:** Slashing through the jungle; striking gold.
- **cita:** "entrepreneurship is the path of uncertainty like slashing your way through the jungle"
- **terminos:** the eternal known; new digital nine to fives
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-016-207
- **fuente:** How To Escape Wage Slavery (Entrepreneurship Is Modern Survival).md, 15:07-15:54, 2023-08-01
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-023-187
- **tipo:** concepto
- **titulo:** Entrepreneurship is the path of uncertainty, like slashing through a jungle: learn untaught skills, accept failure, rejection and slow progress
- **desarrollo:** "Entrepreneurship is the path of uncertainty, like slashing your way through the jungle". You're required to learn skills not taught in school, be okay with failure, rejection and slow progress, learn from mistakes, show up again tomorrow and push until you strike gold — "required in other words to be human". As an entrepreneur you hunt for survival by gathering knowledge, creating a valuable product and giving it to people who'd benefit. If you have a bad relationship with money or business: people actually want to buy products that benefit their lives; if a product hurts people, obviously don't sell it, "but that's not all that business is".

### U-024-231
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Entrepreneurship is the only logical option for long-term thinkers: the mind craves challenge, ladders and vacations become boring, and people don't believe good work exists
- **desarrollo:** People climb ladders like the corporate ladder because the mind craves challenge, but they get used to it and it becomes boring; vacation and retirement likewise. You need the next challenge, usually involving work — but most hate their work, so they don't think good work exists, or that they can create it, pursue their life's work and enjoy and control it. "Entrepreneurship is the only logical option for long-term thinkers. It's the path of uncertainty requiring skills not taught in schools. You must be okay with failure, rejection, and slow progress." "The secret is cultivating a skill set so impactful that you can't help but share it. You solve your own problems, sell the solution, and improve humanity. That's entrepreneurship." "Employment isn't our natural state. Your psyche is wired to hunt. But today's threats are psychological and spiritual, not physical. Nobody wants to be a monkey in a cubicle."
- **ejemplos:** Corporate ladder; monkey in a cubicle.
- **cita:** "you solve your own problems, sell the solution, and improve humanity. That's entrepreneurship."
- **terminos:** life's work; wired to hunt
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental

### U-015-133
- **tipo:** historia
- **titulo:** The web design job: learning programming as a backup, procrastinating desk work, and fearing comfort and autopilot
- **desarrollo:** He studied programming because he learned he could do it without a degree, as a backup plan, trusting he was good at self-educating. He got a "very base level web design job at an agency in Arizona," spending the first hours of the day on his own stuff. "The brutal reality of working a desk job": most of the work is procrastination; most people (with exceptions) can work on their own stuff, procrastinate until the end, then copy-paste templates (for the e-commerce stores he served) and get paid. He knew that staying too long he'd get comfortable — "the wife, the mortgage, the kids, the responsibilities" (nothing wrong with them) — which would "pile drive me into the state of not having any time, money, or energy," and life would "go on autopilot."

### U-012-205

- **desarrollo:** After a few months in a job, once trained, the challenge plateaus: clock in, do tasks, clock out — and you're somehow exhausted. At the web design agency, while desperately trying to get out, he stopped wanting to go to the gym although health and fitness had been a lifelong love; the first time he skipped was after work, exhausted from sitting in a cubicle all day — "the fluorescent lights above me were just sucking the life out of me". You get bored; it's against your nature; attention shifts to "what else could I be doing?", which for most means opening the phone and "rotting your brain". Rarely does a job require consistent skill improvement to match greater challenge.

- **desarrollo:** He'd been in the gym his whole teenage years; it was very important to him. At his 9-to-5 web design job it was the only time he started skipping, since going after work reduced his free time further. Schedule: 1-hour commute, leave at 8, arrive 9, clock out at 6 (an hour lunch doesn't count toward the 9-to-5), drive 10 minutes to the gym, work out maybe an hour, drive back (an hour), then go to bed to sleep and live healthily — health was a value then. "That sounds horrible."

### U-014-138
- **tipo:** historia
- **titulo:** The job as a ticking clock: delaying with loans, procrastinating at work to build freelance web design
- **desarrollo:** Once he got the job he felt "the clock would start ticking down toward my demise": getting a job was "the bane of my existence." He stayed in a fifth year of college taking out loans to delay having to get a job, frantically trying business models; from observation he believed once employed it was downhill: responsibilities stack up, you fall into "the conventional life path." Thanks to experimentation and curiosity he found his way: at the job he procrastinated on work until the last minute to focus on freelance web design, sensing he was on the verge. After a few pivots it worked: typical websites, clients from referrals, then a more specific system for a more specific target based on skills he meshed together (similar to what he teaches now). Still, as a full-time freelancer he hated working on projects not his own — which is when he came to social media.

# SECCIÓN 2.4 — La psique cazadora en un cubículo


## CLÚSTER C-T01b-28: Monkeys in a cubicle
- idea: Símil (no insulto): tu psique está cableada para cazar y el mundo físico cambió más rápido que la mente; quedarte demasiado en un 9-a-5 (o en un "new 9 to 5": agencia o freelance) es ser "a monkey who lives in the jungle trapped in a fluorescent lit box". Sin riesgo ni lo desconocido no hay descubrimientos ni dopamina buena; el emprendedor "caza" para sobrevivir (conocimiento, producto valioso, ponerlo frente a quien lo necesita). Versión 2024: un chimpancé en un cubículo no prosperaría; el aburrimiento repetitivo se acumula tan lento que operas en un "Lower State of Consciousness" y lo crees normal ("of course this is for some jobs"). Versión 2022: no estamos hechos para ser monos en cubículos; estamos cableados para ser emprendedores, que son creadores, y las plataformas permiten conectar con tu comunidad.
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: ninguna (la metáfora se mantiene 2022–2024 con matices crecientes)
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-012-007
- **tipo:** metáfora
- **titulo:** The monkey in a cubicle: a hunting psyche trapped in a fluorescent-lit box
- **desarrollo:** Argument chain: (1) "your psyche is wired to hunt"; (2) the physical world has changed much faster than our minds can adapt — we have the same neurobiological processes as our ancestors; (3) so if you stay in a 9-to-5 too long you become "like a monkey who lives in the jungle that is trapped in a fluorescent lit box" — he insists it's a simile, not an insult. (4) Stuck in a 9-to-5 or a "new 9 to 5" (an agency or freelancing) you live in the Eternal known; "you can't make new discoveries in the known"; you get bored, depressed and see life as meaningless because the only dopamine comes from superficial sources. (5) You never take risks or push into the unknown to discover new knowledge, tools and potentials "that raise dopamine levels in the brain". (6) As an entrepreneur you "hunt for your survival" by gathering knowledge, creating a valuable product and putting it in front of people who could benefit.
- **ejemplos:** agency or freelancing labelled as a "new 9 to 5"
- **cita:** "you are like a monkey who lives in the jungle that is trapped in a fluorescent lit box is that healthy for the monkey"
- **terminos:** monkey in a cubicle, new 9 to 5, the Eternal known, hunt
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-012-005
- **fuente:** The Future Of Work Is Play (How To Create A Digital Career).md, 5:48–7:28, 2023-12-31
- **tension:** ninguna

- **tipo:** metáfora
- **titulo:** "Monkeys in a cubicle": ancestral mental wiring in a new environment; slowly accumulating boredom makes a lower state of consciousness feel normal.
- **desarrollo:** Dan Koe: "if you were to put a chimpanzee in a fluorescent lit cubicle for 8 hours a day do you think it would Thrive of course not it lacks the natural environment that contributes to its psychological well-being you don't need a scientific paper to assume this." "Most people are monkeys in a cubicle they have the mental wiring that our ancestors did but they're in a new environment and nobody understands how to act within that environment." "The psychological effects that come with boredom and a repetitive string of tasks slowly builds and builds and because it slowly builds... you don't even notice it so you're operating at this Lower State of Consciousness for so long that you just think it's normal," you don't think you can feel better or that any opportunities are worth pursuing. At such a job "there is no more learning there is no challenge there is no growth" (he notes "of course this is for some jobs").
- **ejemplos:** A chimpanzee in a fluorescent-lit cubicle.
- **cita:** "most people are monkeys in a cubicle"

- **origen:** propia

## CLÚSTER C-T06-04: Hunting (the monkey in a cubicle and becoming your own dopamine dealer)
- idea: La psique no está diseñada para la vida moderna: un mono en un cubículo sufre. Necesitamos "cazar", que hoy significa buscar fuentes de dopamina novedosas y significativas (descubrir algo, tomarlo y crear con ello). Si la vida repetitiva de autómata te parece normal, estás atrapado en la Matrix. Por eso el autor pide no subcontratar la curiosidad (no pedirle recomendaciones: eso no es cazar) y "convertirte en tu propio dealer de dopamina": la gente que no actúa son "dopamine junkies" que no saben reemplazar sus golpes actuales por el negocio. Receta: tener una idea vaga de lo que quieres, construir cualquier cosa hacia ello, aprender lo que más te apetezca para obtener tu dosis y cambiar si te aburres.
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: U-016-219 (2023) recomienda aprender "websites, logos" por la dosis de dopamina, mientras que en 2024 (U-016-021, fuera de este bloque) los llama cosas que no hacen falta al empezar.
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): FUSION: U-003-168 <= U-003-107 | mismo pasaje del "monkey in a cubicle" repetido literalmente en la compilación "Get Ahead Of 99% Of People With Deep Work & Monk Mode" (2024) a partir de "Change Your Life In 6 Months" (2023)

- **desarrollo:** "Your psyche isn't wired for Modern Life... not wired for being under fluorescent lights going and sitting in a cubicle." "Think of it like a monkey in a cubicle... a monkey would suffer in a cubicle because its psyche is not wired for that." "We need to hunt and in this age what I mean by hunting is novel dopamine sources or meaningful dopamine sources or import[ance] something new in your life discoveries creativity... being able to find something new take it create something with it and that feels so good." If "the repetitive NPC automaton lifestyle" (automaton: "a little robotic doll thing") is normal to you, "you are by definition trapped in The Matrix which is a metaphorical term for you are just enslaved and a slave to routine."

## CLÚSTER C-T15-13: Entrepreneurship is modern survival (the psyche wired to hunt)
- idea: Tesis central del bloque: los ancestros eran "emprendedores" metafóricos que servían a pequeñas comunidades físicas con un rol específico (cocinar, curar, cazar, hacer fuego, cuidar niños); el mundo cambió, pero la psique es la misma, así que esas necesidades se satisfacen hoy mediante el emprendimiento, que es el vessel del propósito. La psique está cableada para cazar y recibe recompensa neurobiológica por cazar su supervivencia, "not for being a monkey in a cubicle". Cazar hoy es descubrir conocimiento, habilidades, ideas y oportunidades para metas propias, crear un producto valioso y ponerlo frente a quien se beneficia; internet es el "hunting ground" y la dopamina, la brújula. Las metas asignadas (exámenes, rutinas laborales, creencias culturales) tienen el problema de que su camino ya es conocido. Ni siquiera el empleo perfecto es tuyo: no puedes cambiarlo ni usar los impulsores intrínsecos (curiosidad, pasión, propósito, autonomía, maestría). Las barreras de entrada colapsaron, pero sigue siendo cuestión de habilidad y tiempo: si los resultados fueran inmediatos se volverían commodity y perderían sentido.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: formulación estable 2023–2026 con capas sucesivas (internet como coto de caza en 2024; la advertencia sobre la IA en 2026). Sin cambio de posición.
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-008-009
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Entrepreneurship is modern day survival: our ancestors served niche physical communities and our psyche remains the same
- **desarrollo:** Chain: (1) our ancestors were entrepreneurs who served their communities; (2) in the past you had a little niche community, physical rather than an unlimited digital one, where you had a specific thing to provide (cook, naturopath who helped people heal, doctor, hunter, fire-maker, caretaker of the kids); (3) things have subtly shifted in tangible terms, but our psyche remains the same; (4) therefore we have to fulfill the same needs through entrepreneurship. Later in the video he adds: your psyche is wired to hunt; you are neurobiologically rewarded for hunting for your survival, "not for being a monkey in a cubicle"; hunt for money to ease survival-induced stress and open room for creativity.
- **ejemplos:** Cook, naturopath, doctor, hunter, fire-maker, child caretaker in ancestral communities.
- **cita:** "your psyche is wired to hunt you are neurobiologically rewarded for hunting for your survival not for being a monkey in a cubicle"
- **terminos:** entrepreneurship is modern day survival; hunt for money
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** ninguno
- **fuente:** The Future Of One-Person Businesses (Take Advantage Now).md, 5:05-6:00 and 18:14-19:04, 2023-03-12
- **tension:** ninguna

- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Even the perfect gig isn't yours: you can't change it or leverage the intrinsic drivers
- **desarrollo:** Humans have a unique ability to create — "the gift of God within us to create," which "feels amazing" — and creating for someone else with an assigned purpose differs; "so much scientific literature... about how the psyche is wired that all point to entrepreneurship" (no specific source given). It's increasingly hard to find the perfect gig, and "even the perfect gig isn't perfect": there'll be something you hate, and because it isn't yours you can't change it, nor leverage the intrinsic drivers that make life good: curiosity, passion, purpose, autonomy, mastery — "not extrinsic, not something being assigned to you."
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "even the perfect gig isn't perfect"
- **terminos:** intrinsic drivers; curiosity; passion; purpose; autonomy; mastery
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-011-010
- **fuente:** Self Improvers Are Creating Their Own Careers (The New Economy).md, 3:53–4:41, 2023-04-16
- **tension:** ninguna



# SECCIÓN 2.5 — Pirámides de dominio, de atención y juegos de estatus


## CLÚSTER C-T01a-20: Society is a pyramid scheme (dominator vs actualization hierarchies)
- idea: Todo se organiza en jerarquías, que son de dos tipos (adaptado de Ken Wilber). Las "dominator hierarchies" tienen estructura de pirámide: muchos con poco poder abajo y pocos con mucho poder arriba. Las "natural/actualization hierarchies" se ordenan por "increasing wholeness" (partículas → átomos → células; letras → palabras → oraciones). La sociedad y sus partes (escuela pública, gobierno, empleo corporativo, algunas instituciones religiosas) son "hierarchies within hierarchies" de dominación. Subir la escalera trabajando duro rara vez lleva a la cima ("10 people at the top and a million at the bottom... bad odds"), así que hay que crear la propia jerarquía de actualización (un negocio propio). A la objeción "someone has to do this job" responde que para eso existe la resolución creativa de problemas: "if enough people do this then the universe will correct itself".
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-023-053
- **tipo:** concepto
- **titulo:** Two types of hierarchy: dominator hierarchies (pyramid schemes) vs natural/actualization hierarchies (increasing wholeness)
- **desarrollo:** Everything is structured as a hierarchy, of two types. (1) Dominator hierarchies, which give the word hierarchy a bad reputation: pyramid-scheme-esque structures. (2) Natural hierarchies or actualization hierarchies: in order of increasing wholeness, where the whole on one level becomes part of the whole of the next. Society mimics hierarchical structure, but society (context dependent) falls under dominator hierarchy. Its parts — public schools, government, corporate jobs, even some religious institutions (more culture, but still part and whole) — are structured this way: "hierarchies within hierarchies". Main feature: more people with less power at the bottom, fewer people with more power at the top; in a true pyramid scheme the investors at the bottom enable this power distribution.
- **ejemplos:** Natural: particles → atoms → cells → organisms; letters → words → sentences → paragraphs. Dominator: public schools, government, corporate jobs, religious institutions.
- **cita:** "there are more people with less power at the bottom and then there are less people with more power at the top"
- **terminos:** dominator hierarchies; natural hierarchies; actualization hierarchies; increasing wholeness; hierarchies within hierarchies
- **origen:** adaptada-de:Ken Wilber
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-023-052
- **fuente:** Society Is A Pyramid Scheme (Take Back Control Of Your Life).md, 7:21-9:06, 2023-01-15
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-023-054
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Climbing the ladder through hard work rarely reaches the top: bad odds, so you must create your own actualization hierarchy
- **desarrollo:** In corporate jobs or school, you're taught to climb the ladder through hard work, usually manual labor. But it's "close to impossible to reach the actual top of the pyramid if you go about it this way"; you usually get stuck at one level: "if there's 10 people at the top and a million people at the bottom... those are just bad odds". Not everyone climbs every ladder (most people aren't trying to become pastors). Those who want out must create their own: start their own business, "create their own actualization hierarchy to escape a dominance hierarchy". These structures are created every day: "they transcend and include after creative emergence".
- **ejemplos:** 10 people at the top vs a million at the bottom; the religious ladder (pastors).
- **cita:** "the people that want to break out of this pyramid scheme as structure have to create their own"
- **terminos:** climb the ladder; actualization hierarchy; dominance hierarchy; creative emergence
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-023-053
- **fuente:** Society Is A Pyramid Scheme (Take Back Control Of Your Life).md, 9:06-9:53, 2023-01-15
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-023-058
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** "Someone has to do this job" is what creative problem solving is for; if enough people leave the traditional route, the universe corrects itself
- **desarrollo:** Responding to the objection that someone has to do these jobs: "this is what creative problem solving is for". If people woke up, wanted a better future and chose not to go the traditional route, they'd have to solve a problem; "if enough people do this then the universe will correct itself". We may go through bad times, but from a universal perspective that doesn't really matter; it's part of the process of life, birth and death.
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "it's like well someone has to do this job someone has to do this it's like that this is what creative problem solving is for"
- **terminos:** creative problem solving
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-023-057
- **fuente:** Society Is A Pyramid Scheme (Take Back Control Of Your Life).md, 13:16-14:01, 2023-01-15
- **tension:** ninguna



## CLÚSTER C-T01a-22: The collective societal vision (status games)
- idea: La visión social colectiva para tu futuro es sacar buenas notas, quizá un empleo bien pagado, ver TV para calmar la mente y quizá jubilarse feliz a los 65, todo impulsado por la búsqueda inconsciente de estatus, supervivencia y aprobación. Por condicionamiento se vuelve hábito: imitamos, "we become a product of others", perseguimos status games (autos de lujo) e invertimos atención todo el día para recuperar ese dinero, sin consultar nunca nuestros propios valores, visión, deseos, necesidades e impacto. La salida tiene dos pasos: (1) hacerse consciente; (2) crear la propia actualization hierarchy que ordene la mente, con metas o problemas de creatividad, plenitud e impacto crecientes.
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): FUSION: U-003-129 <= U-023-059 | mismo pasaje de la "collective societal vision"; U-003-129 (compilación de 2024) es más completo porque incluye la salida en dos pasos y la cita de apertura

### U-003-129
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** The collective societal vision (good grades, high-paying job, TV, retire at 65) runs on unconscious status, survival and approval seeking; escape by awareness plus your own actualization hierarchy.
- **desarrollo:** "The collective societal vision for your future is to get good grades maybe get a high-paying job watch TV to ease your mind and maybe retire happily at 65 both with the unconscious seeking of status survival and approval." Through conditioning it becomes habit; "we go on to mimic others... we become a product of others," chase status games, buy fancy cars, invest attention all day to make the money back, and "never think to consult or create and then consult our own values Vision wants needs and potential impact." Escape: (1) "becoming aware" (which he's doing now); (2) "creating your own actualization hierarchy to order your mind," which presents goals — i.e., problems — increasing in creativity, fulfillment and impact, potentially spreading to others. Work your way up, solving problems over and over; that's where you invest attention. Opener quote: "nobody I want to be trained into a job I hate work for 40 years and get angry at a TV until I die 90% of the population does exactly that wake up it's not too late" (attribution unclear; read as a pull-quote).
- **ejemplos:** Fancy cars; retiring at 65.
- **cita:** "we never think to consult or create and then consult our own values Vision wants needs and potential impact on the world"
- **terminos:** collective societal vision; status games; actualization hierarchy
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-003-128
- **fuente:** Get Ahead Of 99% Of People With Deep Work & Monk Mode.md [1:40:14; 1:47:47–1:49:22] (2024-03-21)
- **tension:** ninguna

- **desarrollo:** "The collective societal vision for your future is to get good grades, maybe get a high-paying job, watch TV to ease your mind and maybe retire happily at 65", all with the unconscious seeking of status, survival and approval. Through conditioning it becomes habit; we mimic others, become a product of others, chase status games, spend money on fancy cars, invest attention all day to make that money back, and never think to create and consult our own values, vision, wants, needs and potential impact on the world.
- **ejemplos:** Fancy cars; retiring at 65.

## CLÚSTER C-T01b-17: The system is rigged (learn the rules of the game)
- idea: "The system is rigged, you can't change it but you can learn it, that is how you rig yourself in the system's favor." El camino convencional ya está programado en la psique colectiva por la "curse of knowledge" (en su uso propio: aprendemos sin cuestionar lo aprendido), de modo que jugamos un juego diseñado por otros, en general para mantener el juego; "90% of the time it just doesn't work out". No puedes liberarte al instante: primero aprendes las reglas del juego y luego creas las tuyas sin romper las otras. Señal de época: "the programmers can't patch the game fast enough" (trabajo remoto, creator economy frente a currículos escolares, cripto, freelancers en lugar de empleados).
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: en 2022 el sistema está amañado y lo usas a tu favor; en 2025 la sociedad está "rigged against you" (U-023-212, C-T01b-02). Ver Evolución.
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): FUSION: U-023-230 <= U-003-023 | mismo pasaje "the system is rigged, you can't change it but you can learn it" del video de 2022, repetido en la compilación de 2024; U-003-023 solo agrega el matiz "90% of people or however many percent"

### U-023-230
- **tipo:** principio
- **titulo:** "The system is rigged, you can't change it but you can learn it — that is how you rig yourself in the system's favor"
- **desarrollo:** The macro game of life: in an open-world strategy game like World of Warcraft, common patterns are stacking gold, choosing a profession, leveling up your character and the progression with those — the familiar "life is a video game" metaphor, which he takes deeper. "The system is rigged, you can't change it but you can learn it, that is how you rig yourself in the system's favor". The conventional path to success is programmed into the collective psyche before we know it, "due to the curse of knowledge" (in his usage: humans wanting to know and learn more, learning being the foundation of human experience, and not questioning what we learn). So we pursue winning a game others laid out, usually in favor of maintaining the game itself. "90% of the time it just doesn't work out": the "secure" path ends in too much anxiety, stress, overwhelm, trapped in "this jail or prison of their own doing".
- **ejemplos:** World of Warcraft: gold, profession, leveling.
- **cita:** "the system is rigged you can't change it but you can learn it that is how you rig yourself in the system's favor"
- **terminos:** macro game of life; curse of knowledge (used idiosyncratically); collective psyche
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-023-229
- **fuente:** How I Turned My Life Into A Video Game.md, 4:32-6:07, 2022-12-11
- **tension:** "Curse of knowledge" is used with a meaning different from its usual sense (the author's own usage; ambiguous). Also contrasts with U-023-225/U-023-212 where society is "rigged against you".


### U-003-023
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** "The system is rigged — you can't change it but you can learn it": the conventional path is preprogrammed into the collective psyche and mostly serves to maintain the game.
- **desarrollo:** "The system is rigged you can't change it but you can learn it that is how you rig yourself in the system's favor." The conventional path to success "has already been programmed into the collective psyche before we know it" due to what he calls the "curse of knowledge" (see U-003-024): humans keep learning and do not question what they learn, so before we know it we are pursuing a game others laid out, "usually in favor of maintaining the game itself" — being trained into the game to keep it going. And "90% of the time it just doesn't work out that way": the path thought to be secure leaves "90% of people or however many percent" with too much anxiety, stress, overwhelm, "trapped in this jail or prison of their own doing."
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "the system is rigged you can't change it but you can learn it that is how you rig yourself in the system's favor"
- **terminos:** the system is rigged; collective psyche; prison of their own doing
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-003-021
- **fuente:** Get Ahead Of 99% Of People With Deep Work & Monk Mode.md [12:50–13:38] (2024-03-21)
- **tension:** The "90%" figure is self-hedged ("90% of people or however many percent").


### U-010-156
- **tipo:** principio
- **titulo:** You can't break free instantly: first learn the rules of the game, then create your own rules without breaking the others
- **desarrollo:** "You can't break free in an instant because that's just not how things work. First, you learn the rules of the game and then you kind of create your own rules of the game without breaking the other rules." You play the game others want for you, as you do in society learning from your parents, "but then eventually you reflect and you question": what can I actually do, how can I create my own game and get better results than those before me?
- **ejemplos:** Learning from parents, then questioning.

### U-003-025
- **tipo:** opinión
- **titulo:** "The programmers can't patch the game fast enough": signs that the conventional system's flaws are revealing themselves.
- **desarrollo:** Times are changing: more people go remote and do their own thing; "the flaws in the system itself are revealing itself and the programmers can't patch the game fast enough" ("who are the programmers I have no idea"). School curriculums can't keep up with the creator economy, which educates people on doing their own thing rather than being trained into the conventional employment system that school is tied to. In banking, whatever your beliefs about Bitcoin/crypto/decentralized currency, someone saw a problem and individuals/small teams are building solutions. Freelancers with digital skills are hired by big companies and individuals as contractors rather than employees. These are the people "actively trying to solve the problem of this faulty system."
- **ejemplos:** Remote work; creator economy vs school curriculums; crypto/decentralized currency; freelancers replacing employees at agencies.
- **cita:** "the flaws in the system itself are revealing itself and the programmers can't patch the game fast enough"

- **nivel:** complementario
- **prerrequisitos:** U-003-023
- **fuente:** Get Ahead Of 99% Of People With Deep Work & Monk Mode.md [13:38–14:31] (2024-03-21)
- **tension:** ninguna



## CLÚSTER C-T01b-18: Society is a pyramid scheme (dominance hierarchy vs actualization hierarchy)
- idea: En estas estructuras hay más gente con menos poder abajo y menos gente con más poder arriba; en empresa o escuela te enseñan a subir la escalera con trabajo duro, pero con "10 people at the top and a million at the bottom... those are just bad odds" y sueles estancarte. Historia: la civilización se construyó con tribus esclavizando tribus y esa dinámica se abstrajo en empleo, ley y cultura; es matemáticamente imposible que todos estén arriba. La salida no es abolir las jerarquías sino crear la tuya: "create their own actualization hierarchy to escape a dominance hierarchy" (empezar un negocio). Escapar de la esclavitud moderna tiene dos pasos: (1) tomar consciencia, (2) crear tu jerarquía de actualización, una serie de metas-problemas crecientes en creatividad, plenitud e impacto.
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: ninguna (2023–2026 consistente; en 2026 aclara que no se dejan las jerarquías en general)
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-003-126
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Society as a pyramid scheme: climbing a dominator hierarchy through hard work gives bad odds (10 at the top, a million at the bottom); to escape, create your own actualization hierarchy.
- **desarrollo:** In these structures "there are more people with less power at the bottom and then there are less people with more power at the top," and in a true pyramid scheme "there are investors at the bottom that enable this power distribution." In a corporate job or school "you're taught to climb the ladder through hard work and usually manual labor," but it's "close to impossible to reach the actual top" — "if there's 10 people at the top and a million people at the bottom... those are just bad odds" — so you usually get stuck at one level. Those who want out "have to create their own": start their own business, "create their own actualization hierarchy to escape a dominance hierarchy." These hierarchies "are created every single day" — they transcend and include after creative emergence.
- **ejemplos:** 10 people at the top vs a million at the bottom; not everyone climbs the religious ladder (few become pastors).
- **cita:** "if there's 10 people at the top and a million people at the bottom how are you those are just bad odds"

- **desarrollo:** "We escape this modern enslavement one by becoming aware" (which the video aims to do) "and then the second thing is by creating your own actualization hierarchy to order your mind and create a better future for yourself and those around you". An actualization hierarchy presents a series of goals, which are problems, increasing in creativity, fulfillment and impact along the way, that may spread to others. ## CLÚSTER C-T01b-19: Society is a pyramid scheme of attention (unconscious destruction)
- idea: "Society is a pyramid scheme of attention": lo que no recibe atención no existe; dejar de atender a quien crees ser es la ego death de las enseñanzas espirituales. Las megacorporaciones solo existen porque muchos abajo dan atención a tareas, visiones y valores; "attention is the root of all of it". Por naturaleza no todos los de abajo pueden subir (la atención debe llenarse desde abajo), por eso los empleos son "stepping stones" o algo entre lo que saltar. Primera realización: contribuyes a una "unconscious destruction" (cajeros de McDonald's, programadores militares, diseñadores web para estafadores: "forgive them for they know not what they do").
- nivel: avanzado
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

## CLÚSTER C-T10b-20: The status game (status and value)
- idea: Todos persiguen estatus: rechazar el juego es elegir estatus bajo, y creer que no lo persigues por nobleza sigue siendo perseguirlo. La gente asigna estatus al verte y decide por él si te da oportunidades; incluso los grandes líderes espirituales e intelectuales juegan ese juego. Si la búsqueda es inconsciente, manipularás sin saberlo; hecha consciente, puede beneficiar a otros. Estatus y valor van juntos: sin valor, el estatus es una ilusión que engaña más de lo que persuade. Jugado a sabiendas (auto, vestimenta, lugares) abre redes y oportunidades sin volverte esclavo de ellas; online, la prueba social la dan tu marca y tus ideas.

- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-027-241
- **tipo:** principio
- **titulo:** You are chasing status: rejecting the status game is choosing to be low status; claiming not to chase it is still chasing it
- **desarrollo:** "You can reject the status game but by doing so you choose to be low status"—decreasing impact, increasing suffering, and never realizing you still chase status. It's "an immutable principle in human nature" since the dawn of time. Thinking you don't chase status as a noble pursuit is still chasing status—like the desire to kill the ego is still a desire. That's not bad, it's natural; once you accept it you begin to be rewarded. People judge you by perceived status; not realizing this means accepting a life of being treated unfairly with close to zero impact.
- **ejemplos:** desire to kill the ego.
- **cita:** "to think that you aren't chasing status as a noble Pursuit is still chasing status"
- **terminos:** status game; low status; immutable principle
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** ninguno
- **fuente:** The Value Equation How To Become A High-Status Individual.md, 0:00–0:52, 2024-03-17
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-027-242
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Everyone has a status in every circle; people assign it on sight and decide opportunities on it—even glorified thought leaders play the status game
- **desarrollo:** You have status in your social circle, job/career, religious organization. People assign status when they first see you (out of your control), but you can position yourself to increase the likelihood of being perceived high status. People decide whether to engage, offer opportunities, hire, pay or help you based on it. Leaders we glorify—Jesus Christ, Eckhart Tolle, Naval Ravikant, Alan Watts, Jordan Peterson, Ken Wilber, any thought leader—play a status game whether they say so or not (Koe doubts it's unintentional given their awareness): how else would they attract large followings perceived as high status and change the direction of evolution? He rejects edge cases of high-status people who neglect inner work: status is one part, not the whole; don't pursue it at all costs; other things balance a good life. "Money doesn't buy happiness" doesn't mean don't try—otherwise you close yourself to the experiences of playing the game of life and pushing further toward depth and meaning.
- **ejemplos:** Jesus Christ, Eckhart Tolle, Naval Ravikant, Alan Watts, Jordan Peterson, Ken Wilber.
- **cita:** "they are playing a status game whether they say they are or they aren't"
- **terminos:** perceived status; position yourself; Game of life
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-027-241
- **fuente:** The Value Equation How To Become A High-Status Individual.md, 0:52–2:27, 2024-03-17
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-027-243
- **tipo:** advertencia
- **titulo:** Unconscious status-seeking makes you manipulate and cheat without knowing it—become conscious of it to make it beneficial
- **desarrollo:** Contributing to humanity is the prerequisite to getting something from humanity, so status matters. Because status-seeking is natural, if it remains unconscious "you will manipulate and cheat without knowing it you will be the cause of evil without knowing it," because you won't learn the inner workings of your mind that let you turn the status you create into something beneficial for others. Even the "Consciousness scale" is a hierarchy—status: telling him to reach the highest level of absolute love is still persuading him that it's worth pursuing. He's not saying the scale is wrong; it's a tool, an ideology, a construct to move in a better direction—as is understanding status.
- **ejemplos:** Consciousness scale.
- **cita:** "you will be the cause of evil without knowing it"
- **terminos:** Consciousness scale; unconscious status
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-027-241
- **fuente:** The Value Equation How To Become A High-Status Individual.md, 2:27–3:16, 2024-03-17
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-027-244
- **tipo:** principio
- **titulo:** Status and value go hand in hand: without value, status is an illusion that deceives more than persuades
- **desarrollo:** The purpose of becoming high status is getting what you want from life. To become high status you must become valuable, "or else that status is Just an Illusion and you're deceiving more than you are persuading" people to give you what you want. Quote from his book The Art of Focus: "the path of the problem solver or value Creator is how you escape the world of replaceability fall in love with the challenge that problems present from superficial to metaphysical and your ideal future will create itself." Value is created by solving problems, creating solutions, forging your own path and creating something unique with the story you carve out.

### U-026-226
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Status games knowingly played open networks and opportunities: the matte black Mercedes GT63 S, dressing well, nice locations
- **desarrollo:** "I drive a fairly nice car that's the one thing that I allow myself to like splurge on": "a Mercedes gt63 S it's pretty fast it sounds good it's matte black so it's on brand but it is a status game and I'm aware of that and I feel like if people are aware that they're playing status games then it helps you not become a slave to those things." The car, the way you dress, nice locations, living somewhere nice give "access to these this network and opportunities that are just given to you if you look good". Contrast: if you look "like a homeless guy because you don't care in your hyper-spiritual hippie but you're struggling to make money and survive and deep down you actually hate your life nobody's going to come up and talk to you". If you're presentable and "show that you invest in how people perceive you", people approach; "if a billionaire comes up and talks to you and gives you a million dollar opportunity that's available to you now". "That's more of a luck game but it is a game and it affects your mindset it's abundance being able to spend this money knowing that you have the resources and knowledge and education to make it back."
- **ejemplos:** Mercedes GT63 S; billionaire approaching; hyper-spiritual hippie.

# SECCIÓN 2.6 — Del paradigma industrial a la era de la información


## CLÚSTER C-T01a-09: Mimetic grind culture (visible vs invisible extreme)
- idea: Somos "mimetic creatures": imitamos para sobrevivir y para no quedar fuera de la familia o de una cultura obsesionada con la productividad ("our mind takes the shape of that which allows us to fit in"). La cultura glorifica el "visible extreme" (el fenómeno que facturó $1M en tres semanas "just from grinding"), que es un highlight reel de "unconscious competence", y esconde el "invisible extreme": el contexto y la fórmula que de verdad generan los resultados (por ejemplo, la distribución). Debajo hay miedos y deseos ocultos que atan a la gente al grind: romantizar el camino largo, querer probarse ante quien no le importa, el miedo a ganar dinero que viene de una familia frugal y creer que todavía no se merece leverage.
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-019-123
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Humans are mimetic; culture glorifies the visible extreme (grind highlight reels) and hides the invisible extreme
- **desarrollo:** Humans are mimetic creatures: we imitate to survive and avoid being cast out of family or our productivity-obsessed culture; "our mind takes the shape of that which allows us to fit in". Problem: everyone glorifies the "visible extreme": the young startup phenom who built a $1M business in three weeks "just from grinding" feels relatable and confirms what we've been told (harder and longer = more likely to succeed). Wrong: there's a deeper formula. A social media highlight reel driven by "unconscious competence" is not reality. You can spend 10,000 hours writing 10 books that never see more than 50 readers, while someone who knows how to distribute a subpar book sells millions because they focused on what gets results. People miss the context: "the invisible extreme" that actually led to the success of most creatives, visionaries and strategists.
- **ejemplos:** $1M in three weeks startup phenom; 10 books with 50 readers vs subpar book selling millions.
- **cita:** "our mind takes the shape of that which allows us to fit in"
- **terminos:** mimetic creatures; visible extreme; invisible extreme; unconscious competence; highlight reel
- **origen:** propia (mimetic idea used without attribution)
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** ninguno
- **fuente:** Harsh Truth You Don't Need To Grind 247 To Be Successful.md [5:45-7:08] (2025-05-04)
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-019-129
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Hidden fears and desires tying people to the grind
- **desarrollo:** Most people have underlying fears and desires tying them to the grind: (1) they romanticize the long and hard route; (2) underlying trauma makes them want to prove themselves to people who won't or don't care; (3) raised in a frugal family, they delay success because deep down they're afraid to make money, so they work longer hours to act like they're doing something; (4) they think they don't deserve leverage yet, so they do everything themselves or everything at once. Also, it's no longer socially acceptable to say you found and exploited a shortcut. Stopping overwork brings boredom and feels like falling behind.
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "they think they don't deserve leverage yet"
- **terminos:** grind; leverage
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-019-118
- **fuente:** Harsh Truth You Don't Need To Grind 247 To Be Successful.md [11:52-12:38] (2025-05-04)
- **tension:** ninguna



## CLÚSTER C-T01a-15: The industrial paradigm (industrial values)
- idea: La mayoría sigue con una cosmovisión del paradigma industrial: especializarse, conseguir un empleo que exige una cadena mecánica de tareas durante 40 años y jubilarse cuando el dinero ya no se puede aprovechar bien. Por eso tener múltiples intereses se vive como una debilidad. Ese camino se cree seguro, pero "it's the least safe and secure route". Los "industrial standards" te dejan ciego al sistema que te paga ("all you know is how to fill a role in someone else's thing") y te atrapan en un "stress loop" que estrecha la mente. Todos tienen una estrategia, pero a la mayoría se la asignaron ("be humble, don't stick your nose out"), y el nuevo paradigma (IA, información) exige otra. Adam Smith ya advertía que una vida de operaciones simples vuelve a la persona "as stupid and ignorant as it is possible", aunque el autor aclara que no está contra el capitalismo.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: "industrial paradigm" es vocabulario de 2026, pero formaliza ideas que el autor ya planteaba desde 2022 (crítica a la ruta escuela-empleo-jubilación, U-011-003). Ver E-07.
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-010-330
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Society calls multiple interests a weakness because of the industrial paradigm: specialize, perform mechanical tasks for 40 years, retire too late
- **desarrollo:** "Society has convinced you that that trait ... of loving learning or having multiple interests is a weakness," and you may believe it because you spend all day in rabbit holes "and frankly you don't really have much to show for it. All you have is a bit of knowledge." It's understandable: "most people still have a worldview influenced by the industrial paradigm. They fully believe that becoming a specialist, getting a job that requires you to perform a mechanical string of tasks for 40 years, and retiring at an age where your money can't be put to great use outside of buying material objects because you were deprived for so long, is still the best route." Dan "can confidently tell you that that is not the best route."

- **terminos:** industrial paradigm
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental

### U-012-208
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Industrial standards (specialize, high-paying job) leave you blind to the system that pays you — and the stress loop narrows the mind
- **desarrollo:** Most were raised with industrial standards: become a specialist, study one domain hard, get a high-paying job so friends think you're successful. So you stayed blind to most of the process: you understood the one skill, "but you didn't try to understand the system that paid you"; you didn't dedicate time to other domains, so "all you know is how to fill a role in someone else's thing", and your ability to think is crushed. Even if smart in your skill and decently paid, you don't feel financially stable, so you get caught in a chaotic stress loop; "stress narrows the mind", making a life of building your own thing unfathomable. Stuck in the thirds (sleep, work, exhausted), you have no capital saved, no time or energy for personal development, and are too tired "spiritually, not just physically" to re-educate yourself, because most of your waking life fuels someone else's vision.

### U-022-113
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Everyone has a strategy — most were assigned it: the industrial-paradigm default path, habitual by age 20.
- **desarrollo:** The average person doesn't solve problems; they "live the same day over and over again because that's what the conditioning process led to." Parents and teachers "injected values and beliefs into their head that put them on the default path heading toward the goal that society wanted for them, which is go to school, get a job, retire at 65… the industrial paradigm." "By the time they turn 20, it had become so conditioned… it's just habitual… unconscious." "Everyone has a strategy, but for most people, it's not one that they created. It's one that they were assigned": be a good student, make a decent amount of money, "be humble, don't stick your nose out. Your dreams aren't worth pursuing because they're risky, which doesn't make sense because risk is the only way to get reward." Strategic thinking moves you to "a more advantageous position where their problems aren't so painful… of a lesser magnitude… more fun and creative problems to solve. You start to see life as a puzzle rather than a death sentence." The new paradigm (AI, information) "requires a completely different strategy whether or not you agree"; the assigned strategy was "only somewhat beneficial to the old way of life, the American dream."
- **ejemplos:** "Go to school, get a job, retire at 65."
- **cita:** "Everyone has a strategy, but for most people, it's not one that they created. It's one that they were assigned."

- **origen:** propia

## CLÚSTER C-T01a-23: The illusion of certainty (the safe path is the least safe)
- idea: Como nadie puede conocer el futuro con certeza, la masa acude a empleos seguros y sistemas de creencias: "an illusion of certainty to avoid struggle, it's a trap". La forma más rápida de arruinarse la vida es seguir el plan de otro: no cuestionar, tomar la ruta que parece segura y es la menos segura, y evitar el ensayo y error ("the greatest mistake is not making any mistakes at all"). La mente desea orden y busca comodidad, lo que crea un bucle: comodidad → incomodidad → más comodidad, hasta quedar en un pozo ("if it's so comfortable why do you have to numb the pain with quick pleasure?"). Krishnamurti: cuando la mente está segura "it is in decay" (entropía); "you cannot make discoveries in the known". Quien intenta salir mete un pie en el agua y vuelve atrás por miedo o por sobrecarga, pero "to find clarity you have to battle through the chaos". En el fondo la gente sabe que disfrutar la vida es una elección y lo delega en las promesas exageradas de la escuela y el empleo.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna (la tesis se mantiene de 2023 a 2025)
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): FUSION: U-025-029 <= U-026-126 | el autor repite casi literal "it's an illusion of certainty to avoid struggle" en otro video, sin ángulo nuevo; U-025-029 conserva además "mistakes are your light in the dark"

### U-027-214

- **desarrollo:** "The fastest way to ruin your life is to follow someone else's plan." Problem: the mind craves order; without thinking we seek security, safety and comfort. When young, his fate was in parents' and teachers' hands; too young to trust his own thinking, he followed them—the only path he knew—and that's where things go wrong. "The path of least risk is the greatest risk of all"; "a life of comfort is the most uncomfortable thing in the world." - **desarrollo:** Koe quotes Krishnamurti: tradition becomes our security, and when the mind is secure "it is in Decay." You can be happy at times, but deep fulfillment is different; most haven't experienced it, so don't search, and project insecurities by claiming fulfillment working "as a robot would"—"the lines of code are engraved on their brain."

## CLÚSTER C-T01b-21: Historical lens on society (technoeconomic bases, transcend and include, pathological postmodernism)
- idea: Para entender la sociedad actual da "lecciones de historia": las sociedades se clasifican por su base tecnoeconómica (forrajera, hortícola, agraria, industrial, informacional) y quien más produce se vuelve la deidad de esa sociedad; el arado provocó el "rise of the patriarchy", ocio, escritura y matemáticas. El mundo físico cambió algo, pero el mental cambió por completo: cada creación física exige 10–1.000 ideas e iteraciones; la consciencia "transcended and included" lo biológico y el mundo mental es más caótico pero más creativo. Hoy el pensamiento posmoderno "has largely gone pathological" (DEI, política de género): desmantelar todas las jerarquías es imposible porque decir que todas son malas ya forma una jerarquía de valores. Encuadre de Krishnamurti: "It is no measure of health to be well-adjusted to a profoundly sick society."
- nivel: complementario
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-018-020
- **tipo:** framework
- **titulo:** The five technoeconomic bases of society: foraging, horticultural, agrarian, industrial, informational
- **desarrollo:** To understand the "cheap dopamine epidemic," Dan gives "a brief history lesson." Societies can be categorized by their "technoeconomic base," "in a nutshell their basic means of production" (the horse-drawn plow vs. machine farming; the things we use to manage our basic needs). There are five: (1) Foraging: hunting and gathering; men hunted, women gathered. (2) Horticultural: simple planting, where women produced most of the food and the society's deities became female. (3) Agrarian: animal-drawn plows; women couldn't continue farming due to increased miscarriages, so roles changed and deities became male; 80% of agrarian societies relied on male slave labor. (4) Industrial. (5) Informational. The pattern Dan highlights: "whoever produced the most foods or whoever produced the most in general, whether it was male or female, became the deities of that society." Dan does not attribute this framework to a named thinker in this transcript (the terms "technoeconomic base," "great chain of being," "ascending/descending" and "physiosphere/biosphere/noosphere" suggest an integral-theory source, but the transcript doesn't name one; ambiguous).

# SECCIÓN 2.7 — Awareness: el sexto sentido curativo


## CLÚSTER C-T01a-31: Awareness (awareness begets awareness; awareness is curative)
- idea: La awareness es el "sixth sense" y la base de la creatividad: percibir lo que los cinco sentidos no captan (un sentimiento, una idea) y que el "materialist paradigm" no valora. Es secuencial: "awareness begets awareness", como el mapa de un videojuego, donde no puedes ver lo que no exploraste y cada zona desbloquea la siguiente. Por eso, si no te adentraste en lo desconocido, "you understand less than .1% of reality" y sigues atrapado en necesidades de supervivencia sin acceso a las de crecimiento. "Awareness begets improvement, and improvement begets awareness of the next but better problem". Además, "awareness is curative": hay que entrenarse para hacer una pausa ante la tensión o el impulso de reaccionar. Notarlo basta para que la conducta se corrija con el tiempo, aunque al principio no salga perfecto. De Anthony de Mello (recomendado por JK Molina): disolver mediante la awareness los rasgos y creencias adoptados por condicionamiento cultural.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna (formulaciones complementarias de 2022 a 2024)
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-026-007
- **tipo:** metáfora
- **titulo:** "Awareness begets awareness": the unknown is like the unexplored map of a video game
- **desarrollo:** "Awareness begets awareness thoughts beget thoughts ideas beget ideas." You cannot become aware of something "up here" if you were not first aware of the something "here". He compares the unknown to a map in a video game: areas you have not explored are not available to you; you are not conscious of them, you don't know what is there and can only assume. You have to reach one location before you reach the next, and eventually you create a network you can tap into with your awareness, to pull from, to be creative in your endeavors and to expand your mind toward new perspectives and "new realities in a sense". Consequence: progress in awareness is sequential; each step of awareness unlocks the next.
- **ejemplos:** Video game map with unexplored areas.
- **cita:** "awareness begets awareness thoughts beget thoughts ideas beget ideas"
- **terminos:** awareness begets awareness; the unknown; network
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** ninguno
- **fuente:** How To Create A Better Life For Yourself.md, 4:51-5:43, 2022-06-12
- **tension:** ninguna

## CLÚSTER C-T01a-32: The conscious decision-maker (figure it out for yourself)
- idea: "There is one extremely important skill and that is figuring it out for yourself": hacer lo que quieres desde una mente consciente, no desde una "mediocre mind" que obedece. Eso exige preguntarse qué deseas, qué fuerza de supervivencia te empuja hacia eso, si es bueno y si encaja con tus valores. A veces hay que hacer lo que otros piden para sobrevivir, pero en paralelo se necesita un proyecto propio hacia la autonomía y la maestría. Los que rinden de verdad son simplemente "conscious decision-makers" que se conocen y cambian lo que no da progreso cueste lo que cueste. Las historias de los ídolos (Marcus Aurelius, Alejandro Magno, Alan Watts) son highlight reels; "it's all self-awareness all the way down" (Dickie Bush). El hábito infantil del autor de observar las acciones de la gente (cestas del súper, rutinas) para discernir adónde llevan, y pasar del juicio al discernimiento ("do I want that or not?"), muestra cómo se practica.
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-001-081
- **tipo:** principio
- **titulo:** The one extremely important skill: figuring it out for yourself and doing what you want from a conscious mind, not a mediocre one.
- **desarrollo:** "There is one extremely important skill and that is figuring it out for yourself, not doing what people tell you to do, and doing what you want from a conscious mind" — not a "mediocre mind" that says "I can do whatever I want, you want me to jump off a bridge, okay." Ask: what are your desires; what survival mechanism or force pulls you toward them; is it good or bad; do you actually want it; does it align with your values; do you have values? What people tell you won't be conducive to what you want; you may have to do it for survival, but you must pursue a project of your own that leads to autonomy and mastery full-time, not for someone else. Many of his aims were deemed impossible by closed minds projecting opinions. Therefore, to pursue mastery and autonomy, you need a skill that monetizes your interests and passions — which leads into his argument for writing.
- **ejemplos:** "Jump off a bridge."

### U-012-002
- **tipo:** historia
- **titulo:** The author's childhood habit of observing people's actions to discern where they lead
- **desarrollo:** Since childhood the author was "obsessed with finding a better way of living". Everywhere he went he observed people's actions — what they put in their grocery baskets, their daily routine (go to work, go home, watch Netflix, fall asleep, repeat) — to discern whether doing those things would lead to the life they had, and whether he wanted that life. He asked what, how and why: what were they pursuing, how did they get where they are, why did they look that way. He admits there was some judgment as a teenager ("we all judge people it's okay") but says he wants to shift that from judgment to discernment. The method: with enough data from observation, decide "do I want that or do I not want that".
- **ejemplos:** People who "don't really push into the unknown": work, home, Netflix, sleep, repeat. A person picking up "a 1,000 calorie dessert drink for breakfast with a sandwich that leaked grease from its wrapper".

Aplicación cotidiana: decidir por observación de patrones sociales (lo que alguien pone en su carrito del supermercado: "¿quiero terminar así?"), sin juicio; mal uso: observar solo para justificar lo que te dijeron que hicieras.

- idea: Para actuar sobre una posibilidad primero hay que ser consciente de ella.
